Detailed definitions in additive notation:
- For a monoid M, \(M^{\rm gp}\).
- For \(x,y\), \(x \prec y\) if there exist integers m,n with \(my \le x \le ny\). Note in additive notation, \(my\) means m times y; if m negative, it's in group.
- Sharp: if \(z,-z\in M\) then z=0.

Proof infinitesimal injective:
Let \(p:M→N\) infinitesimal. Suppose \(y\in M\) and \(p(y)=0\). Put \(x=-y\in M^{\rm gp}\). Then \(p(x)=0\in N\). We have \(x\prec y\): take \(m=-1\), \(n=0\), because \(-y\le -y\le 0\). Also \(p(y)\prec p(x)\) because both are 0 and \(0\prec 0\). Infinitesimal condition gives \(x=-y\in M\). Sharp gives y=0. Thus p injective.

Key lemma proof as above.

Construct Q_+:
Let \(P=\mathbb{N}^3\) with generators \(e_1,e_2,e_3\). Define
\(Q_+ = (P\oplus \mathbb{N}d)/(d+e_3=2e_2)\) (saturated; actually already).
\(Q_- = (P\oplus \mathbb{N}d)/(d+e_2=2e_3)\).
Let \(f_\pm:P→Q_\pm\) be canonical.

We might denote the extra generator \(d_+\) and \(d_-\) to avoid confusion.

Now verification:

Case \(a\ge c\): Use Q_+. Define \(g_+:Q_+→V\) by \(g_+(e_i)=v(e_i)\) and \(g_+(d)=2a-c\). This is well-defined. Then \(g_+ f_+=v\). We claim f_+ birhizomic at g_+. Let \(P \xrightarrow{h} M \xrightarrow{p} V\) with p infinitesimal and \(p h = v\). Define \(u:Q_+→M\) by \(u(e_i)=h(e_i)\) and \(u(d)=2h(e_2)-h(e_3)\). By Lemma, \(u(d)\in M\). The relation holds. Then \(p u=g_+\), \(u f_+=h\). Uniqueness by p injective. So birhizomic.
Case c>a symmetric with Q_-.

Thus family is birhizomic cover.

Now site/sheaf:

Then \(π_*π^{-1}F(b)=0\), while \(F(b)=Z\). Since \(R^0π_*=π_*\), the induced map on H^0 at b is zero \(Z→0\), not isomorphism. Therefore \(F→Rπ_*π^{-1}F\) is not a quasi-isomorphism.
